## R56 - Frame Snapshot Staging

Problem: The frame job still held DataVault guard bits for `ShinobuAmbientEntitySnapshot`, `ShinobuAmbientAupSnapshot`, and `ShinobuBoidStateSnapshot` for the whole scheduled job. These buffers are derived frame snapshots used by spatial query/presentation, not independent truth owners. Keeping them in the long guard made the vault treat derived query payload as live mutation-owned memory for the full Burst chain.

Solution: Added persistent owner-local `NativeArray` scratch for entity, AUP, and boid-state snapshots. `TryResolveFrameJobBuffers` now opens only live truth/counter buffers from DataVault and hands the job owner-local snapshot arrays. After `FinishFrameJobCompletion` releases the scheduled mutation guard in `finally`, `WriteSpatialGridPayloadAfterRelease` invalidates committed ranges, copies all three snapshots through one-buffer `try/finally` windows, copies entries, and publishes final ranges last. The ranges-last commit marker is the only public validity gate; `SpatialHashQuery` resolves views but accepts a cell only after `range.Flags == Frame`.

Rejected Alternatives: Blindly publishing snapshots without invalidating ranges first was rejected because an external query could see a new snapshot under an old valid range. Keeping snapshots in the scheduled guard was rejected because these are derived frame outputs, not source authority. Moving live entity/AUP/boid truth or counters out of the guard was rejected in this loop because counters need a per-index merge/version protocol and live truth still needs a stronger transaction/staging design.

Scalability potential: Low devices now hold a 4-bit frame guard instead of 7, and spatial query readers fail closed during publish contention. Middle devices keep deterministic query visibility through final ranges. High and Ultra devices keep continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling for neighbor samples, probe count, update stride, cell size, and render density; no binary quality switch was added.

Hardware Impact: Three snapshot buffers leave the long scheduled frame lease. New work is three contiguous unmanaged `MemCpy` windows after simulation settlement, each guarded by one `TryLockBuffer`/`TryUnlockBuffer` pair in `finally`. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because build/profiler execution was throttled.

## R57 - Spatial Range Bootstrap Clear Flattening

Problem: `ClearSpatialGridRangeTable` was still clearing `ShinobuSpatialGridBucketRanges` with a managed `for` loop while the DataVault buffer lock was held. The loop contained only default assignments, but it still held the lock across one branch and one indexed store per range row during lifecycle rebinding.

Solution: Converted the method to `unsafe` and replaced the per-row clear with one contiguous `UnsafeUtility.MemClear` under the same single `TryLockBuffer` / `TryUnlockBuffer` window. The view-open, created, and length checks remain inside the lock because they validate the exact mapped view before touching the pointer; all exit paths still release through `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Moving `ShinobuAmbientEntities`, `ShinobuAmbientAups`, `ShinobuBoidStates`, or `ShinobuEcosystemCounters` out of the scheduled frame guard was rejected in this loop. `ShinobuFloraFaunaSymbiosisSolver` borrows ambient entity/AUP handles and snapshots them from live DataVault rows; `AbyssalSwarmTunerWindow` reads live ambient rows for editor vectors. Counters also receive cold writes by index. Staging these facts without versioned merge/commit semantics would be a cross-domain data contract change, not a safe lock-shortening patch.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a bootstrap/rebind lock window with per-row stores. Middle devices keep deterministic range invalidation. High and Ultra devices keep the continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` ecosystem scaling already present in probe count, neighbor solve weight, cadence, and render density; this change adds no binary `isLowEnd` switch and no new physical simulation.

Hardware Impact: Critical section work changed from `SpatialGridBucketRangeCapacity` indexed default writes to one contiguous clear. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because the final build gate sampled CPU at `96`.

## R58 - Symbiosis Link Publish Copy Flattening

Problem: `ShinobuFloraFaunaSymbiosisSolver.TryWriteSymbiosisLinks` held the `ShinobuSymbiosisLinks` DataVault lock while copying link rows from a prebuilt managed scratch array through a `for` loop. The data preparation was already outside the lock; the remaining issue was per-row managed-array indexing and NativeArray indexing inside the critical section.

Solution: Converted the method to `unsafe`, computed `requestedCount` before lock acquisition, skipped the lock for no-work publishes, and replaced the per-row loop with a single `UnsafeUtility.MemCpy` from a pinned `SymbiosisChemicalLinkDTO[]` to the locked NativeArray view. The lock still wraps only the target view resolve and the byte copy, and release remains in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Clearing the target tail was rejected because the previous method did not clear tail rows and adding that would change the semantic contract of this cold editor/CSV publish route. Converting the managed scratch array to a persistent `NativeArray` was rejected in this loop because it would broaden lifecycle ownership; the current patch removes the lock loop without changing scratch ownership. Touching live ambient truth guards was rejected again because symbiosis consumes those rows through borrowed handles.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor sessions avoid row-by-row link publication under a DataVault lock. Middle devices preserve deterministic symbiosis link payloads. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling in the symbiosis solver; this is a memory-copy route cleanup, not a binary tier switch or added simulation.

Hardware Impact: Critical section work changed from `writeCount` indexed assignments to one contiguous copy of `writeCount * 16` bytes. The source struct layout is explicit `Size=16` with offset assertions for `FloraHash/FaunaHash/ChemicalTransferRate/Flags`, so the byte copy is layout-equivalent to the old loop. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because no profiler/build was launched.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid row-by-row counter publication under a DataVault lock. Middle devices keep the same deterministic counter payload. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` flocking behavior; saved CPU time buys headroom for presentation density already driven elsewhere, not a new binary quality switch.

## R60 - Construction Socket Lookup Clear Flattening

Problem: `HabitatConstructionManager.ClearSocketLookupForInvalidation` can hold `IntegritySocketLookupBufferId` while `ClearSocketLookup` clears every `SocketLookupSlot` with a `for` loop. This is outside AI, but still inside the batch domain: GlobalDataVault lock duration across first-party scripts. The old helper also ran for owner-local scratch, so the fix needed to preserve both call sites.

Solution: Added `Unity.Collections.LowLevel.Unsafe` and converted `ClearSocketLookup` to a bounded unmanaged clear. The helper now returns for uncreated or empty arrays, then calls `UnsafeUtility.MemClear` over `lookup.Length * UnsafeUtility.SizeOf<SocketLookupSlot>()`. The DataVault caller keeps its `try/finally` release unchanged.

Rejected Alternatives: Leaving construction untouched was rejected because this is a direct DataVault critical-section loop with a trivial byte-clear equivalent. Editing broader construction graph algorithms was rejected because socket graph semantics belong to the construction domain, not this lock-duration pass. A managed temporary clear buffer was rejected because it would violate zero-GC and add owner confusion.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid per-row socket lookup clearing while a vault writer lock is held. Middle devices keep identical invalidation semantics. High and Ultra devices do not gain new gameplay truth; saved CPU/lock budget remains available for richer construction preview presentation elsewhere under continuous quality scaling.

Hardware Impact: Critical section work changed from `lookup.Length` indexed default assignments to one contiguous clear of `lookup.Length * 48` bytes. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because build/profiler execution was blocked by CPU and active dotnet process.

## R61 - VR Somatic Comfort Import Lock Split

Problem: `SomaticTunerWindow.ImportComfortCsv` held DataVault write locks too broadly for editor import. It locked `ShinobuVRSomaticCsvScratch` while doing file IO, then acquired `ShinobuVRSomaticProfile`, then optionally acquired `ShinobuVRSomaticProfileLookup`; parsing could therefore run with up to three write locks held. This violated the lock-flattening requirement even though the route is editor-only.

Solution: Moved CSV bytes into a cold editor `byte[4096]` scratch before any profile/lookup lock. Added a hash-staging parser overload in `VRSomaticProvider` so profile rows can be parsed under only the profile lock, then released. Built the lookup table in a cold editor `VrComfortProfileLookupSlotDTO[8]` scratch outside DataVault ownership. The lookup lock now performs only one `MemClear` and one `MemCpy`, then releases in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the DataVault CSV scratch lock was rejected because file IO under a vault writer lock is unnecessary editor staging. Parsing profile and lookup in one call was rejected because it requires two target writer locks at once. Re-parsing CSV under the lookup lock was rejected because it would shorten lock depth but still do heavy parsing inside a critical section. Adding a new runtime authority route was rejected; this is editor import staging only.

Scalability potential: Low devices and busy editor sessions avoid multi-lock import stalls. Middle devices keep identical profile/lookup payloads. High and Ultra devices gain no new gameplay truth; saved lock budget protects presentation and simulation systems from editor contention. No binary quality branch was introduced.

Hardware Impact: Critical section shape changed from file read + CSV parse + profile write + lookup clear/insert while multiple locks can be held, to: file read outside locks; profile parse under one lock; lookup `MemClear/MemCpy` under one lock. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because build/profiler execution was blocked by CPU and active compiler processes.

## R62 - Buoyancy Blackbox Event Phase Split

Problem: `BuoyancyDisplacementRuntime.FinishPendingSolverCompletion` released the scheduled job guard, then `TryLatestCounterHasFault` read `BuoyancyCounterDTO` through `ResolveVaultBuffer` without a new ownership window. The old `DumpBlackBoxOnce` also resolved telemetry/cursor and pushed `GlobalTelemetryBus` event/dump from that unguarded post-release path. `WriteCompletedSimdUtilizationTelemetry` could call blackbox push while still holding the SIMD telemetry mutation guard.

Solution: Replaced `TryLatestCounterHasFault` with `TryReadLatestCounterFault`, which acquires only the counter guard bit, copies scalar fault payload into locals, and releases in `finally`. Replaced `DumpBlackBoxOnce` with `PushBuoyancyFaultEvent`, which has no DataVault access. `WriteCompletedSimdUtilizationTelemetry` and editor `GenerateMockSimdBenchmark` now compose SIMD fault payload under guard and call `PushSimdFaultEvent` only after the guard release.

Rejected Alternatives: Re-reading the telemetry ring under a two-buffer telemetry/cursor guard was rejected because the counter already carries the required non-finite flag/count and last entity hash. Leaving event/dump calls inside the guard was rejected because blackbox routing is presentation/diagnostic work, not memory ownership. Removing the blackbox route entirely was rejected because the fault evidence path is still useful when it can run after release.

Scalability potential: Low devices no longer spend blackbox/event routing time while holding buoyancy telemetry ownership. Middle devices retain counter-based fault detection. High and Ultra devices keep the existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` buoyancy and SIMD quality math; no binary tier switch or new physical simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Removes unguarded post-release counter read and moves blackbox push outside completion guards. Runtime microseconds are unmeasured. Static lock proof: counter fault read is one guard bit; SIMD event push runs after `ReleaseMutationGuard`.

## R63 - Ecosystem CSV Profile Import Lock Split

Problem: `TryLoadProfilesCsvCold` and `TryLoadCarrionProfilesCsvCold` used DataVault CSV scratch buffers as editor import staging. That meant file IO and CSV parse work lived in the same ownership route as DataVault-managed scratch/profile mutation. The old profile guard masks included both CSV scratch and profile buffers, so editor imports could hold unnecessary vault ownership while doing cold managed IO/parse.

Solution: Moved CSV bytes into cold editor static scratch arrays guarded by `Interlocked.CompareExchange`. Parsing now happens into cold editor DTO scratch before any DataVault write lock. The only DataVault writer window left in each import method is the profile buffer commit: resolve the target `NativeArray<T>` view, copy bytes with `UnsafeUtility.MemCpy`, release in `finally`. The static scratch busy flag is released in an outer `finally` with `Volatile.Write`, so contention fails closed and no caller waits on shared scratch memory.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the CSV scratch BufferID in the guard was rejected because DataVault is cross-domain native ownership, not a file import heap. Re-parsing under the profile lock was rejected because it would still put parser work inside a critical section. Removing the existing CSV scratch handles from lifecycle registration was rejected because other audit/self-audit paths still reference those BufferIDs and the safe cleanup needs a separate contract pass.

Scalability potential: Low devices and busy editor sessions avoid writer-lock stalls during CSV IO and parse. Middle devices preserve deterministic profile payloads. High and Ultra devices get no new gameplay truth; saved lock budget protects simulation/presentation phases. No binary quality switch was added; existing ecosystem systems keep continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling for cadence, density, and visual workload.

Hardware Impact: Critical section shape changed from CSV scratch/profile ownership around import staging to a single profile buffer byte copy. Nutrient commits only `parsed * sizeof(NutrientProfileDTO)` bytes. Carrion commits `CarrionProfileCapacity * sizeof(CarrionDecayProfileDTO)` bytes to preserve the old parser's clear-tail behavior. ## R64 - Metabolism CSV Import And Suit Index Lock Split

Problem: `TryLoadBiologicalProfilesCsv` and `TryLoadSuitThermalProfilesCsv` leased `MetabolismCsvScratchBuffer` together with target profile buffers while doing editor file IO and CSV parse work. `TrySetSuitProfileIndex` also used the two-buffer suit selection mask even though direct index writes do not read `MetabolismSuitThermalProfilesBuffer`.

Solution: Removed `MetabolismCsvScratchBuffer` from both import mutation masks. Added cold editor static byte/DTO scratch arrays guarded by `Interlocked.CompareExchange`; file IO and parse work now happen before any DataVault mutation guard. The biological import commits parsed `MetabolicSpeciesRuleDTO` rows with one `UnsafeUtility.MemCpy` into `MetabolismSpeciesRulesBuffer`. The suit import commits the full staged `MetabolicSuitThermalProfileDTO` capacity with one `MemCpy`, preserving the previous parser's clear-tail behavior. Added `SuitProfileIndexMutationGuardMask` for direct index writes.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping DataVault CSV scratch was rejected because DataVault is native ownership, not a cold file import heap. Parsing under the profile lock was rejected because it keeps heavy scan work inside the critical section. Splitting `TrySetSuitProfileHash` was rejected because it needs profile-hash resolution plus index write to remain coherent; a safe split needs a versioned profile hash map or cached immutable lookup.

Scalability potential: Low devices and busy editor sessions avoid vault-writer stalls during CSV IO/parse and avoid unnecessary thermal-profile locking for direct suit index writes. Middle devices keep identical profile/index payloads. High and Ultra devices gain lock budget for richer presentation/simulation elsewhere; no binary quality branch or physical simulation was introduced.

Hardware Impact: Critical section shape changed from CSV scratch/profile ownership around file IO and parser scan to one target view resolve plus one byte copy. Direct suit index writes changed from a two-buffer mask to one index-buffer mask. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because compilation/profiling was not launched.

Reusing `ShinobuMacroEcosystemCsvScratch` for file IO was rejected because the scratch buffer is a DataVault-owned native resource, not a cold editor heap. Splitting the scheduled `JobMutationGuardMask` was rejected in this loop because the macro population/diffusion/telemetry job owns a coherent multi-buffer transaction and needs a staged job-buffer design, not a surgical CSV import fix.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor sessions avoid unowned DataVault mutation and cold IO/parser work touching shared native memory. Middle devices preserve deterministic biome spec payloads. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` macro scaling in diffusion steps, integration substeps, and quality flow weight; no binary quality branch or physical simulation was introduced.

Scalability potential: Low devices and busy editor sessions avoid unowned native writes and avoid file IO/parser work touching DataVault memory. Middle devices preserve deterministic bay/rule payloads. High and Ultra devices keep the existing visual respawn fade and telemetry behavior; this change only buys lock safety and does not add a physical simulation or binary quality switch.

## R67 - Buoyancy CSV Import Guard Split

Problem: `BuoyancyDisplacementRuntime.TryLoadMaterialVolumesCsv`, `TryLoadMaterialSettlingProfilesCsv`, and `TryLoadSimdMathTolerancesCsv` held DataVault writer guards while reading files into `ShinobuBuoyancyCsvScratch` and parsing CSV. The SIMD path also held tolerance and hydrodynamic tuning buffers together while applying tolerance-derived tuning. This was editor/cold, but it still violated the DataVault rule: native vault memory is not a file import heap, and file IO/parser loops do not belong inside writer guards.

Solution: Removed `ShinobuBuoyancyCsvScratch` from all three CSV mutation masks. Added cold editor static staging arrays guarded by `Interlocked.CompareExchange`: one `byte[65536]`, `BuoyancyMaterialVolumeDTO[2048]`, `BuoyancyMaterialSettlingProfileDTO[512]`, and `SimdMathToleranceDTO[64]`. File IO and parser work now complete before any DataVault guard. The publication phase is four one-buffer commits: material volumes, settling profiles, SIMD tolerances, and SIMD hydrodynamic tuning. Every commit releases in `finally`. Parser APIs gained `Span<T>` overloads so the old NativeArray parser contracts remain intact while the new path stages without vault scratch.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the CSV scratch BufferID in the guard was rejected because it only lengthened lock hold and gave no authority benefit. Re-parsing CSV under each target lock was rejected because it would still keep parser loops in critical sections. Holding SIMD tolerance and tuning in one guard was rejected because tuning can be derived from staged rows while owning only the tuning buffer. Removing the existing `_csvScratchHandle` lifecycle was rejected because editor tools and handle readiness still reference the BufferID and that cleanup needs a separate compatibility pass.

Scalability potential: Low devices and busy editor sessions avoid vault writer stalls during CSV file IO and parser scans. Middle devices preserve identical material volume, sleep profile, and SIMD tolerance payloads. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` buoyancy/SIMD math; the saved lock budget protects simulation cadence and diagnostic tooling rather than adding binary quality switches or new physical simulation.

Hardware Impact: Critical section shape changed from file read + CSV parse + target writes under one multi-buffer guard to cold managed staging plus one target `MemCpy` per table. SIMD tuning still loops over at most 64 staged rows while holding only `ShinobuSimdHydrodynamicTuning`; it no longer holds tolerance and tuning together. ## R68 - Analytical Gerstner Cold Boot And Telemetry Guard Split

Problem: `AnalyticalGerstnerWaveRuntime.EnsureColdBooted` used one broad cold boot mutation guard for spectrum, tuning, telemetry cursor, profiles, counters, and in editor CSV scratch. It performed CSV parse, profile/mock spectrum generation, counter clearing, and tuning writes while holding the guard. `PostFixedTick` then used another broad telemetry guard for tuning/results/counters/telemetry ring/cursor, scanned up to 1024 results inside that guard, and could call `GlobalTelemetryBus` while the guard was held.

Solution: Replaced cold boot broad ownership with one-buffer commit masks. CSV bytes stage in editor-only cold `byte[65536]`; parsed profile rows use a caller-owned `stackalloc WaveSpectrumProfileDTO[32]`, eliminating the static DTO scratch race. Spectrum rows use `stackalloc GerstnerWaveParamsDTO[2]`; profile and mock spectrum loops run before any writer guard. Tuning, spectrum, profiles, telemetry cursor, and counters each commit under one target guard with `finally` release. Telemetry is now built from `TryReadOnlyHandle` snapshots outside writer guards, then published as two one-buffer commits: ring entry, then cursor. Blackbox event push runs after commits.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping cold boot broad because it is rare was rejected; it still violates the one-lock proof and can stall startup during compaction. Holding profile DTO rows in a static staging array was rejected after self-audit because releasing the scratch busy flag before commit allows another editor route to overwrite rows. Keeping telemetry scan under a writer guard was rejected because 1024 result reads and bus calls are not direct mutation. Removing `Shinobu263WaveCsvScratch` lifecycle was rejected because compatibility cleanup needs a separate route audit.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid boot and post-solver stalls caused by broad writer guards and result scans. Middle devices retain identical wave spectrum, tuning, and telemetry payloads. High and Ultra devices keep continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` wave math; no binary quality branch or physical simulation was added. Saved lock budget protects solver cadence and presentation sync.

Hardware Impact: Cold boot changed from one five-buffer writer section plus optional CSV scratch to staged stack spans and five one-buffer commits. Telemetry changed from one five-buffer writer section plus 1024-row scan and blackbox bus calls to pure read-only staging plus two single-buffer writer sections. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because compilation/profiling was not launched.

## R69 - Vehicle Damage CSV And Blackbox Guard Split

Problem: `VehicleComponentDamageRuntime.TryLoadCsvLayout` held one mutation guard spanning `Shinobu vehicle damage` CSV scratch, grid write, grid read, and tuning buffers while doing editor file IO, CSV parse, and grid copy work. `DumpBlackBoxIfFaulted` also leased both state and telemetry ring buffers even though the method only used state fields before pushing a global blackbox event.

Solution: Replaced the import guard with cold editor scratch staging plus three one-buffer commit routes. The CSV parser gained a `Span<VehicleGridCellDTO>` overload so parser loops run outside DataVault ownership. `BlackboxReadMutationGuardMask` now owns only `StateReadBuffer`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `CsvScratchBuffer` in the import guard was rejected because DataVault native memory is not an editor file heap. Holding grid write, grid read, and tuning together was rejected because their commits are independent after the CSV has been staged. Rebuilding the entire scheduled `DamageMutationGuardMask` was rejected in this loop because the fixed-tick job transaction legitimately pins coherent grid/signal/state/telemetry buffers until `PostFixedTick`; splitting that path needs a double-buffer/job ownership redesign.

Scalability potential: Low devices and busy editor sessions avoid vault writer stalls from CSV file IO and parser scans. Middle devices preserve identical vehicle damage grid and tuning payloads. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` vehicle hazard scaling; saved lock budget protects simulation cadence and diagnostics, not a new binary tier switch.

## R70 - Submarine Boot, CSV Import, And Gyro Guard Split

Problem: `SubmarineDynamicsRuntime.TryInitializeBootProfiles` held config, drag LUT, state, control, mass, and hull profile ownership while doing legacy file IO, drag LUT fill, and per-vehicle default math. CSV override/hull-profile/gyro-profile import paths also used broad target guards or DataVault CSV scratch memory while reading and parsing editor files. `TryInitializeGyroDefaults` held gyros, profiles, and counters together while iterating vehicle rows.

Solution: Replaced boot ownership with six one-buffer commit masks. Boot now stages config, drag LUT, states, controls, masses, and hull profiles into stack spans; it publishes drag/state/control/mass/hull first and commits config last as the fail-closed source-hash latch. CSV imports now use cold editor byte/DTO scratch and parse outside mutation guards; target buffers commit separately. Gyro default init now snapshots gyros/profiles/counters read-only, stages rows in stack spans, then commits gyros, profiles, and counter through separate guards released in `finally`.

Reusing `Shinobu332GyroCsvScratch` for editor file bytes was rejected because DataVault is native ownership, not a file import heap. Splitting `SimulationCoreMutationGuardMask` and `GyroScheduleMutationGuardMask` in this pass was rejected because those are coherent fixed-tick job transactions and require a staged/double-buffer design.

Scalability potential: Low devices get shorter boot/import lock windows and fail-closed CSV contention instead of waiting on native scratch. Middle devices preserve identical deterministic config, drag LUT, hull, gyro, and profile payloads. High and Ultra devices retain existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` cadence and telemetry behavior; this pass does not add a binary tier switch or physical simulation.

Gyro default critical section changed from a three-buffer row loop to two stack staging loops plus three one-buffer commits. CSV file IO and parser loops no longer execute under target writer guards. ## R71 - Quest DAG CSV Override Guard Split

Problem: `QuestDagCsvOverrideIngestor` held a six-buffer mutation guard over `QuestDagNodes`, `QuestDagNodeRuntime`, required item hashes/quantities, counters, and CSV monitor while parsing CSV rows, scanning node hashes, and writing diagnostics. That made cold editor CSV ingestion block unrelated quest DAG buffers and put O(rows * nodeCount) hash search inside the guarded region.

Solution: Added a staged `CsvOverridePatch` path. CSV rows are parsed against read-only counters/nodes/runtime and staged in a cold editor static patch array before any writer guard. The apply phase now uses one short mutation guard only for the atomic runtime+item payload (`QuestDagNodeRuntime`, `QuestDagRequiredItemHashes`, `QuestDagRequiredItemQuantities`). `LastCsvRowsApplied` and CSV monitor are committed through separate one-buffer `TryAcquireQuestDagWriteBuffer` calls, each released in `finally`. Shared editor scratch is fail-closed with `Interlocked.CompareExchange` and `Volatile.Write`.

Rejected Alternatives: Splitting runtime, item hash, and item quantity into three one-buffer commits was rejected because `QuestDagResolverJob` reads `RequiredItemStart/Count` together with hash/quantity arrays. Publishing those separately can expose a mixed active quest requirement for one frame, especially when an existing runtime already points at the same slot. Keeping counters and monitor in the broad guard was rejected because they are diagnostic publication, not part of the atomic quest requirement payload. Rewriting the full DAG loader and scheduled resolver guard was rejected for this loop because those are coherent graph/job transactions requiring staged graph publication or double-buffered resolver ownership.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor sessions avoid holding quest DAG memory while parsing CSV and linearly scanning node hashes. Middle devices preserve identical override semantics. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` quest/resolver behavior unchanged; this patch buys deterministic lock budget rather than adding binary quality switches or new simulation.

Hardware Impact: Critical section changed from CSV parser + node hash search + six-buffer writes under one guard to cold staging plus: one short three-buffer atomic payload copy loop, one counter write lock, and one optional monitor write lock. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because compilation/profiling was blocked by CPU policy.

## R72 - Thermodynamics Config Worker Byte Scratch Lock Removal

Problem: `ThermodynamicsHazardGridRuntime.FileWorker.ApplyPendingConfigLoads` copied the binary/CSV worker byte arrays into DataVault byte buffers through `TryCopyWorkerBytesToVault`, then parsed those bytes and committed constants while still holding the byte-buffer write lock. The CSV path also called `TryWriteConstantsWithOwner` before releasing `_csvBytes`, creating simultaneous byte-buffer and constants writer ownership. This is a deadlock/contention vector for a route that only needs one final constants publication.

Solution: Removed `TryCopyWorkerBytesToVault` and all byte-buffer write locks from the file worker path. Binary config now parses directly from `_binaryConstantsWorkerBytes` via `TryBuildBinaryConstants`. Editor CSV config now parses directly from `_csvWorkerBytes` via a byte-array parser overload. `ThermodynamicsCsvBytes` and `ThermodynamicsBinaryConstantBytes` remain lifecycle-opened compatibility lanes, but no parse/apply path writes or locks them. Constants publication stays in `TryWriteConstantsWithOwner`, which owns only `ThermodynamicsHazardConstants` and releases in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping byte-buffer copies for diagnostics was rejected because no local consumer reads those buffers and diagnostics already read constants/telemetry. Taking a mutation guard over byte buffer plus constants was rejected because it would preserve the nested ownership fault. Moving file IO to main thread was rejected because the existing SPSC worker handoff is adequate and does not hold DataVault locks.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid main-thread lock windows around byte copies and CSV parse loops. Middle devices keep identical thermodynamics constants. ## R73 - Shader Telemetry Dump Read-Only Snapshot

Problem: `GlobalShaderDispatcher.DumpTelemetry` acquired `ShaderGlobalStateMutationGuardMask` only to copy telemetry slots into a stack snapshot before writing a dump file. The method did not mutate shader global state, so the writer guard could block the visual-sync path during an over-budget dump.

The dump path now copies telemetry rows through a read-only snapshot into `stackalloc Span<float4>` and then performs file IO outside DataVault ownership. Failure to read marks `TelemetryFlagVaultUnavailable` and still writes an empty/fault-marked dump.

Resolving mutable slots without a guard was rejected because read-only handle APIs exist. Moving dump file IO into a job was rejected because this is crash/diagnostic I/O, not a frame simulation workload.

High and Ultra devices retain the existing shader visual overkill path and continuous `HomeostasisBrain.GlobalQualityWeight` scaling; this patch does not add binary quality branches.

The owner phase now copies the 300-frame telemetry ring into a preallocated `_telemetryDumpSnapshot`, records frame/reason/count scalars, and queues a cached ThreadPool callback only if `_telemetryDumpInFlight` is clear. `WriteTelemetryDumpWorker` serializes the staged snapshot cold and always clears `_telemetryDumpInFlight` in `finally`.

Creating a dedicated worker thread was rejected because it risks orphan-thread lifecycle complexity. Allocating a fresh managed snapshot per dump was rejected because the route must stay bounded and predictable. Moving file IO to `LateFrameTick` was rejected because it still blocks a frame phase; the cold worker is cleaner.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid fault-time post-simulation file IO spikes. Middle devices preserve identical dump payload. High and Ultra devices keep existing `HomeostasisBrain.GlobalQualityWeight` driven upload cadence/payload scaling; no binary tier branch or physical simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault dump work changed from path creation plus binary serialization of up to 300 telemetry entries to a bounded 300-entry struct copy and one ThreadPool enqueue. Runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because no profiler run was launched.

## R82 - Seed Ship Fault Dump Phase Split

Problem: `SeedShipAnomalyRuntime.FinishFrameJobCompletion` ran in the late-frame completion lane, then called `TryDumpTelemetry` on anomaly budget/non-finite flags. The dump route had already been made read-only relative to DataVault, but it still packed bytes and performed `Directory.CreateDirectory`/`FileStream.Write` synchronously in the frame phase. That is not a write-lock deadlock, but it is a phase stall vector at exactly the point where the system has just exceeded compute budget.

Solution: Added a static cached `System.Threading.WaitCallback`, a preallocated `_telemetryDumpScratch` byte array, `_telemetryDumpInFlight`, and `_telemetryDumpByteCount`. `TryDumpTelemetry` now reads the telemetry ring through the existing read-only vault helper, packs the fixed dump bytes in `PackTelemetryDumpBytes`, and queues `WriteQueuedTelemetryDump`. The worker performs only path/file IO and resets `_telemetryDumpInFlight` in `finally`. No dedicated thread/process is created.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping file IO in `LateFrameTick` was rejected because it hides stalls behind a diagnostic branch. Creating a dedicated thread was rejected because lifecycle/orphan handling is unnecessary for one dump. ## R83 - Repair Tool Blackbox Lock And Dump Split

Problem: `RepairTool.RecordRepairBlackBox` performed counter clamps, state-hash construction, battery normalization, and a `new RepairToolBlackBoxEntry` object initializer while holding `RepairBlackBoxMutationGuardMask`. `DumpRepairBlackBox` was worse: it held the same DataVault mutation guard while building paths, creating directories, opening `FileStream`/`BinaryWriter`, and serializing all 300 blackbox frames. This made a diagnostic route contend with the writer it is supposed to preserve.

Solution: Hoisted the full `RepairToolBlackBoxEntry` construction before the mutation guard and left only current-array resolution plus one indexed assignment inside the guard. Added `TryReadOnlyRepairBlackBox` and changed dumping to copy 300 entries into a preallocated `_repairBlackBoxDumpSnapshot`, then queue a cached `WaitCallback` worker. The worker performs all filesystem work and clears `_repairBlackBoxDumpInFlight` in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping file IO under the mutation guard was rejected because diagnostic dumps are not authority mutations. Allocating a fresh snapshot array per fault was rejected because the path must remain bounded and predictable. Creating a dedicated thread was rejected because it adds lifecycle/orphan risk; CLR ThreadPool with an in-flight gate is enough. Moving hull dent repair locks was rejected in this loop because those writes are gameplay-authority mutation, not blackbox diagnostics.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid blocking the repair blackbox writer on path and disk latency during fault handling. Middle devices keep identical dump payload. High and Ultra devices preserve existing continuous `ResolveRepairQualityWeight`/`GlobalQualityWeight` repair visual scaling; no binary `isLowEnd` branch or new physical simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Writer lock span changed from struct construction plus math plus assignment to direct assignment of precomputed data. Dump lock span changed from 300-entry serialization and file IO under a mutation guard to zero mutation guard in the dump route. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because CPU policy blocked compilation/profiling.

## R84 - Bulkhead Profile CSV Lock Split

Problem: `BulkheadContainmentRuntime.TryLoadProfilesFromCsvFile` acquired `BulkheadProfileImportMutationGuardMask`, resolved both profile and CSV scratch buffers, then performed `FileStream` reads and CSV parsing while the DataVault mutation guard was held. `TryLoadProfilesFromCsvBytes` also parsed bytes directly into the vault profile buffer under the same guard. The CSV scratch buffer had no remaining runtime owner after staging was moved off-vault.

Solution: Added `BulkheadProfileCsvMaxBytes = 8192`, read CSV files into a bounded `stackalloc byte` span before any mutation guard, and changed byte import to parse into a `stackalloc BulkheadProfileDTO` span before acquiring the guard. The guarded section now resolves only `Shinobu220BulkheadProfiles` and copies staged DTO rows, released in `finally`. Removed `_csvScratchHandle` and its `Shinobu220BulkheadCsvScratch` ensure/release/default paths from this runtime.

Parsing directly into the vault profile array was rejected because CSV tokenization, hashing, float parsing, and sanitation do not need memory authority. Allocating a managed byte/profile array was rejected because bounded stack spans are sufficient and avoid GC. Rewriting scheduled bulkhead job pin ownership was rejected because that is simulation authority state, not CSV import staging.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid path/file latency and parser loops while holding a global mutation guard. Middle devices preserve identical profile semantics. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `ResolveBulkheadQualityWeight` / `HomeostasisBrain.GlobalQualityWeight` authority cadence and visual upload behavior; no binary quality branch or physical simulation was added.

## R85 - Hatch Lock Dump, CSV Import, And Write-Lock Flattening

Problem: `BulkheadContainmentRuntime_HatchLocks.DumpHatchBlackBoxIfRequested` held `HatchTelemetryDumpMutationGuardMask` while calling `TryDumpHatchBlackBox`, and that callee performed `Path.GetDirectoryName`, `Directory.CreateDirectory`, `FileStream` creation, and 300-entry telemetry serialization. The same file also used `Shinobu343HatchCsvScratch` as a DataVault byte heap for editor CSV file reads, parsed profiles directly into `Shinobu343HatchProfiles` while the profile write lock was held, and `RefreshHatchLockVaultState` could hold the telemetry cursor write lock while acquiring the tuning write lock.

Solution: Added a cached `HatchBlackBoxDumpWorkerCallback`, a preallocated `_hatchTelemetryDumpSnapshot`, and scalar snapshot fields. The visual-sync dump route now copies telemetry under the mutation guard, releases in `finally`, then queues a ThreadPool worker that owns all path and file IO. Hatch CSV bytes are read into a bounded stack span and parsed into a stack `Span<HatchHardwareProfileDTO>` before `TryCommitHatchProfiles` opens the profile write lane. Removed `_hatchCsvScratchHandle` runtime ownership. Split hatch startup cursor initialization and tuning publication into separate write-lock scopes, each released before the next acquisition.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping file IO under the mutation guard was rejected because diagnostic persistence is not DataVault authority mutation. Keeping the CSV scratch buffer was rejected because file import bytes are not cross-domain truth. Allocating a fresh telemetry/profile snapshot per call was rejected because the routes must stay bounded and predictable. Holding cursor and tuning together was rejected because cursor reset and tuning publication are independent one-buffer writes.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid visual-sync disk stalls under a DataVault mutation guard and avoid editor import/parser loops under profile locks. Middle devices preserve the exact hatch telemetry dump format and CSV profile semantics. High and Ultra devices retain existing continuous `ResolveBulkheadQualityWeight` / `HomeostasisBrain.GlobalQualityWeight` hatch cadence and shader upload behavior; no binary `isLowEnd` branch or new physical simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Mutation guard work for hatch dump changed from 300-entry serialization plus filesystem calls to a bounded 300-entry struct copy and scalar staging. Profile write-lock work changed from CSV parser loop to staged DTO copy. Startup write-lock max outstanding count is now one. Keeping synchronous dump in `LateFrameTick` was rejected because diagnostic file IO can stall the presentation phase and used a DataVault view after guard release. Creating a dedicated thread was rejected because a cached ThreadPool work item avoids orphan-thread lifecycle risk.

Middle devices preserve identical profile rows and H8P3 dump payload. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` particle budget/compute cadence; this pass adds no binary `isLowEnd` branch and no physical simulation.

## R87 - Parasite Target Selection Guard Split

Problem: `ParasiteSwarmGpuRuntime.ResolveTargetSelectionInline` held `TargetSelectionMutationGuardMask` while doing `SignalBus<ThermalSourceSignal>` snapshot traversal, mock target generation, candidate filtering, distance scoring, and top-k insertion. That guard covers `ShinobuParasiteTargets`, `ShinobuParasiteTargetCandidates`, and `ShinobuParasiteTargetCount`; doing all math under it inflated contention for a visual target snapshot. A second fault was found during the split: updating `_lastCandidateOverflowCount` before a successful publish could report new staged overflow while the DataVault still contained the previous targets if the guard was contended.

Solution: Added fixed runtime scratch arrays for `ParasiteTargetCandidateDTO` and `ParasiteTargetDTO`. `StageTargetSelectionScratch` now performs mock/real candidate staging, scoring, and top-k target selection before any DataVault mutation guard. `CommitTargetSelectionScratch` is the only target-selection guarded method; it resolves the three owner views, copies staged DTOs, writes target count, then releases `TargetSelectionMutationGuardMask` in `finally`. Candidate overflow is committed only after the DataVault publish succeeds.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping candidate scoring under the mutation guard was rejected because the math reads only `SignalBus` snapshot data and cached tuning. Scheduling tiny Burst jobs was rejected because the previous path already called `IJob.Execute` inline and scheduling/readback would add coordination without profiler proof. Publishing through a managed event was rejected because GPU upload still requires the existing DataVault-owned target buffer. Clearing the full candidate buffer every frame was rejected because previous semantics only made `TargetCount` authoritative and stale candidate rows were not read by the runtime.

Scalability potential: Low devices now avoid holding a global mutation guard through up to 512 signal scans plus top-k insertion. Middle devices keep identical top-16 thermal target semantics. High and Ultra devices retain the same continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` driven particle budget and mock orbit radius/speed scaling; no binary `isLowEnd` branch and no physical simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: DataVault target-selection guard work changed from signal traversal, candidate math, and top-k ranking to bounded DTO copy plus one count write. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because the current integrator protocol requested static validation rather than compilation/profiling.

Problem: `VoxelSurfaceNetsVault.TryLoadCsvOverrides` used `VoxelSurfaceNetsVaultBufferIds.CsvScratch` as a DataVault byte heap and held `CsvScratchMutationGuardMask` while opening/reading `meshing_profiles.csv`, parsing CSV bytes, and hashing the file. The same file also held `TelemetryDumpMutationGuardMask` across `Path.Combine`, `Directory.CreateDirectory`, `File.Create`, and telemetry binary serialization in `TryDumpBlackBoxOnSlowExtraction` / `TryDumpBlackBox`.

Solution: Removed `CsvScratch` from `VoxelSurfaceNetsVaultHandles`, `VoxelSurfaceNetsVaultBuffers`, creation, resolution, hydration, and the BufferID constants. Dumping now stages the telemetry ring into fixed static `TelemetryDumpSnapshot` while the telemetry lease is held, releases the lease, then queues a cached `WaitCallback`; the worker writes files only from the managed snapshot/scalars and resets `_telemetryDumpInFlight` in `finally`.

Parsing CSV directly inside the tuning writer was rejected because string-free tokenization and hashing are not authority writes. Keeping dump file IO synchronous after releasing the guard was rejected because slow extraction diagnostics can still stall the frame phase. Creating a dedicated thread was rejected because ThreadPool plus an in-flight gate avoids orphan-thread lifecycle risk. Splitting extraction/HZB job leases in the same patch was rejected because those leases protect scheduled job ownership and need a different double-buffer design.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid file IO/parser work under a global mutation guard and avoid disk stalls on slow extraction frames. Middle devices preserve identical CSV tuning semantics and dump binary header/payload format. High and Ultra devices retain existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` tuning, chunk cadence, debug raw capture, and decimation behavior; no binary `isLowEnd` branch or physical simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Removed one DataVault byte scratch buffer from runtime ownership, removed one CSV mutation guard from import, and moved dump file IO out of the telemetry mutation lease into a cold worker. ## R89 - Foundation Snapping CSV Scratch, Profile Guard, And Dump Worker Split

Problem: `FoundationSnappingCalculatorData.TryLoadProfilesFromCsvFile` used `FoundationSnappingCsvScratch` as a DataVault byte heap and included that scratch buffer in `ProfileEditMutationGuardMask`. The editor CSV path held profile edit ownership while reading `FileStream`, walking CSV bytes, parsing tokens, finding profile ranges, clamping offsets, initializing inactive rays, and writing directly into `FoundationSnappingRayOrigins` / `FoundationSnappingProfileRanges`. That violated local scratch ownership and expanded the mutation guard far beyond direct memory publication. A second pass found `DumpTelemetry` still creating directories and writing the telemetry ring synchronously in the caller phase.

Solution: Removed `CsvScratch` from `FoundationSnappingVaultViews`, removed the local `CsvScratchBufferId` route, removed `s_CsvScratchHandle`, and removed the scratch bit from `ProfileEditMutationGuardMask`. `TryLoadProfilesFromCsvFile` now reads CSV bytes into a bounded stack span before any profile write fence or DataVault mutation guard. `TryParseProfilesFromCsvBytes` parses into stack-staged `FoundationRayOriginDTO` and `FoundationProfileRangeDTO` spans with no DataVault ownership. `TryCommitProfileStaging` is the only publication helper; under `ProfileEditMutationGuardMask`, the file route resolves only the two arrays being mutated and copies staged DTOs plus counters before `EndProfileEditLocks` runs in `finally`. Guard contention now records `FoundationPylonFlags.ProfileEditContention` through a bounded atomic CAS path, and `WriteTelemetry` folds the flag into `FoundationTelemetryEntry.Flags` through `Interlocked.Exchange`. `DumpTelemetry` now copies up to 300 entries into a fixed static snapshot, gates duplicate queueing with `s_TelemetryDumpInFlight`, and queues cached `s_TelemetryDumpWorker`; only `TryWriteTelemetryDumpSnapshotCold` touches `Path`, `Directory`, and `FileStream`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `FoundationSnappingCsvScratch` as a compatibility heap was rejected because CSV bytes are import scratch, not cross-domain truth. Calling full `TryReadVaultViews` under the profile guard was rejected because it resolves unrelated module, pylon, telemetry, tuning, SDF, debug, and indirect buffers. Parsing directly into Vault arrays was rejected because tokenization, hashing, float parsing, and offset clamps do not require profile memory authority. Keeping telemetry dump file IO synchronous was rejected because diagnostic persistence can stall the caller phase and does not need DataVault ownership. Creating a dedicated thread/process was rejected because a cached ThreadPool work item avoids orphan lifecycle management. Removing the core enum reservation was rejected in this pass because it is an unused ID reservation, not an active handle/guard route, and broad enum cleanup belongs in a separate memory-catalog pass.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid file IO and CSV parser loops while profile/ray DataVault ownership is held. Middle devices preserve identical CSV semantics and profile/ray capacities. High and Ultra devices keep the existing continuous `HomeostasisBrain.GlobalQualityWeight` tuning path for pylon length, ray count, SDF march steps, radius, shader flare, debug rays, and indirect args; no binary `isLowEnd` branch or new physical simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Removed one DataVault byte scratch handle from Foundation Snapping readiness, removed one scratch bit from the profile mutation guard, and reduced the guarded publication to bounded DTO copies for at most `ProfileCapacity` ranges and `RayProfileCapacity` rays. The added contention proof is one CAS loop on failure and one telemetry `Interlocked.Exchange`; it does not allocate, touch file IO, or extend the profile guard window. Fault dump caller-phase work changed from directory creation plus file write of the telemetry ring to a bounded 300-entry struct copy plus one ThreadPool queue attempt. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because no profiler/device run was performed.

## R90 - Ambient Biota Macro Guard And Fault Dump Split

Problem: `AmbientBiotaDirector.TryHydrateMacroSwarms` and `TryPackMacroHydratedBiota` held `MacroMutationGuardMask` while running inline visual boid hydration/dehydration jobs. Those jobs performed swarm validation, slot search, AUP offset math, velocity/state construction, distance checks, hash reduction, active recount, and signal publication while a DataVault mutation guard was owned. `WriteTelemetryHeartbeat` also staged a fault blackbox dump from the telemetry guard and previously performed the queue decision before the guard was released.

Solution: Added owner-local fixed macro scratch arrays sized during cold Vault readiness. Hydration now stages all visual boid rows into scratch before `MacroMutationGuardMask`; the guard only resolves AUP/velocity/state/counter arrays and copies staged rows with revalidation. Dehydration now stages release slot indices before the guard; the guarded commit clears only still-valid slots and writes counters. Removed `AmbientBiotaMacroHydrationJob` and `AmbientBiotaMacroDehydrationJob` because they were tiny same-frame visual jobs executing under a guard. Telemetry fault dumping now copies the fixed 300-frame ring under `TelemetryMutationGuardMask`, releases in `finally`, then queues the cached ThreadPool worker; the cold writer alone touches `Path`, `Directory`, `FileStream`, and `BinaryWriter`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping inline `IJob.Execute()` was rejected because it bought no scheduling parallelism and expanded a mutation guard with visual-only math. Scheduling a real job was rejected because same-frame schedule/readback would add dispatcher pressure without profiler proof. Allocating per-hydration managed lists was rejected because it violates Zero-GC. Keeping ThreadPool enqueue inside the telemetry guard was rejected because even non-file managed scheduling extends the guard window. Splitting `BiotaJobMutationGuardMask` in this pass was rejected because that guard pins scheduled drift job buffers and needs a double-buffer owner-phase design.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid holding global mutation ownership through swarm loops and fault-time worker enqueue. Middle devices preserve identical macro biomass semantics. High and Ultra devices keep continuous `ResolveMacroVisualQualityWeight01` scaling for visual boid budget/radius/offset/velocity; no binary `isLowEnd` branch and no physical simulation was added. The cinematic cheat remains deterministic shell spawning with cheap polynomial sine/cosine and triangle vertical bias, not particle physics.

Hardware Impact: Macro guard work changed from visual swarm loops plus inline job execution to bounded DTO copy and counter write. Fault telemetry guard work changed to fixed ring copy only; worker queue and file IO occur after release. Exact runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because build/profiler execution was blocked by CPU/compiler policy.

## R91 - Ground Radar Fault Dump Phase Split

Problem: `GroundPenetratingRadarRuntime.DumpBlackBox` opened `GroundRadarTelemetryRing` through a mutable owner-write view and then performed `Path.Combine`, `FileStream`, `BinaryWriter`, and a full 300-frame serialization in the caller phase. The same fault route was triggered from completed radar job publication, so a NaN/fault could turn late-frame completion into a disk stall. `WriteTelemetry` also used a `new GroundRadarTelemetryEntry` value initializer, creating unnecessary text-level audit noise in the hot telemetry writer.

Solution: Added a cached `WaitCallback`, a fixed `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, in-flight scalar gate, snapshot count/cursor fields, and a cold path cache. `DumpBlackBox` now only gates duplicate dumps, copies telemetry through `TryReadVaultBuffer` into the fixed snapshot, and queues the worker. `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` is the only filesystem method and consumes only the staged managed snapshot and scalar cursor/count. `WriteTelemetry` now uses `default` plus explicit field assignments.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the mutable owner view during file IO was rejected because diagnostic persistence is not DataVault authority mutation. Creating a dedicated thread was rejected because the task explicitly forbids orphan processes and a cached ThreadPool callback has bounded lifecycle. Allocating a fresh dump array per fault was rejected because fault handling must be bounded. Moving the file write to `LateFrameTick` was rejected because it would still block a frame phase; the worker isolates disk latency.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a late-frame file IO spike and mutable Vault-view lifetime during a radar fault. Middle devices preserve the exact dump payload shape and radar ping behavior. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous radar fidelity scaling through `ResolveRayCount` and `ResolveRaymarchSteps`; no binary `isLowEnd` branch and no physical subsurface simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault dump work changed from path construction plus binary serialization of the telemetry ring to a bounded 300-entry struct copy and one cached ThreadPool queue attempt. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because build/profiler execution was blocked by CPU/compiler policy.

## R92 - Foveated Render Fault Dump Phase Split

Problem: `FoveatedRenderCommander.DumpBlackBoxOnce` wrote `Dump_1406.bin` synchronously in the runtime fault path. It opened the project or persistent dump path, created directories, wrote a header, then looped 300 telemetry entries while repeatedly opening a read-only DataVault handle per entry. The write lock was already released before this method, but the disk write still lived on the frame/fault caller path and could stall VR presentation under a non-finite foveation state.

Solution: Added a cached `WaitCallback`, fixed `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, in-flight gate, worker fault flag, snapshot cursor/count/sequence scalars, and a cached cold dump path. `DumpBlackBoxOnce` now only gates duplicate dumps, stages telemetry through one `TryReadOnlyHandle`, and queues the worker. `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` serializes the staged managed snapshot only. `TryEnsureBlackBoxDumpPathCold` resolves `Application.dataPath` / `Application.persistentDataPath` on owner phases, so the worker never touches Unity APIs.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping synchronous file IO after the write lock release was rejected because VR fault recovery still runs on a presentation-critical path. Creating a dedicated thread was rejected because the project forbids orphan processes; cached ThreadPool work plus `_blackBoxDumpInFlight` is bounded. Re-reading DataVault per telemetry entry was rejected because one read-only handle snapshot is enough. Reusing `TryAcquireWriteLock` for dumping was rejected because blackbox persistence is diagnostic readout, not authority mutation.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid fault-time file IO and 300 repeated DataVault read-handle probes on the frame path. Middle devices preserve the exact FVRC dump header and 64-byte record payload. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous foveation policy scaling through `RefreshGlobalQualityWeight01`, thermal/GPU pressure, quality relief, and Quest/gaze capability checks; this pass adds no binary low-end/high-end tier switch and no physical simulation.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault dump work changed from path resolution plus file open plus 300 record writes to one 300-entry struct copy and one cached ThreadPool enqueue. The telemetry write path remains one DataVault writer lock released in `finally`; max outstanding write lock is one. Exact runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because no Unity profiler/device run was performed.

## R93 - Procedural Bone Fault Dump And Player Sanitizer Split

Problem: `ProceduralBoneBlenderRuntime.FinishPendingSolverCompletion` held `JobMutationGuardMask` while checking invalid telemetry and calling `DumpBlackBoxOnce`. The old dump path resolved telemetry buffers and called `ProceduralBoneBlackBox.TryDumpTelemetry`, which performed path construction, directory creation, `FileStream`, `BinaryWriter`, and 300-entry serialization synchronously. During the same inspection, `ProceduralBoneBlenderTypes.cs` had `ProceduralBoneSanitizer` under `#if UNITY_EDITOR` even though runtime code calls it from player paths; that is a player compile-risk independent of profiler data.

Solution: Split `FinishPendingSolverCompletion` so it only records `shouldDumpFault` while the job mutation guard is active, releases the guard in `finally`, then calls `DumpBlackBoxOnce` after release. Added a cold fixed `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, cached `WaitCallback`, scalar cursor/count/in-flight fields, and a cold dump root cached from `Awake`, `OnEnable`, and registry replacement. `DumpBlackBoxOnce` now does no file IO and no mutation guard work; it copies telemetry into the fixed snapshot and queues the worker. `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` is the only runtime filesystem method. Moved `#endif` above `ProceduralBoneSanitizer`, removed the unused editor-only `ProceduralBoneBlackBox` synchronous helper, and removed now-dead IO/usings from types.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the dump inside `JobMutationGuardMask` was rejected because diagnostic persistence is not simulation authority. Copying telemetry under the guard and queueing later was rejected because the same 300-entry copy can run after release in the owner phase. Keeping the editor `ProceduralBoneBlackBox` helper was rejected because no current code calls it and it preserved a public synchronous dump route adjacent to runtime types. Launching a Roslyn parser/build under 100% CPU was rejected by the project resource gate.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a fault-time disk stall under a broad animation job guard. Middle devices preserve the exact 300-frame telemetry payload format and procedural bone cadence. High and Ultra devices retain continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` driven solver frequency through `LowQualityUpdateHz`/`HighQualityUpdateHz`, mock AI signal weight, jaw IK, wave amplitude, and trauma amplitude; no binary `isLowEnd` switch and no physical animal-body simulation was added. The cinematic cheat remains deterministic procedural bone waves plus cheap velocity/mock signal modulation.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault handling changed from job-guard-held filesystem serialization to guard-free fixed struct copy plus one cached ThreadPool queue attempt. Player builds no longer depend on `UNITY_EDITOR` for `ProceduralBoneSanitizer`. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because CPU/compiler policy blocked build/profiler/parser execution.

## R94 - Kinetic Character Fault Dump Phase Split

Problem: `KineticCharacterAnimatorRuntime.DumpBlackBoxOnce` still called `KineticCharacterBlackBox.TryDumpTelemetry`, a public synchronous helper in the types file. That helper performed project-root resolution, directory creation, `FileStream`, `BinaryWriter`, and full 300-entry serialization directly from the runtime fault path. The solver mutation guard was already released before the call, but the fault route could still stall the animation/presentation frame with disk IO and kept a reusable public synchronous blackbox route next to runtime DTO types.

Solution: Added a fixed owner-local `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, `_blackBoxDumpInFlight`, cached `WaitCallback`, and cold dump root cache. `DumpBlackBoxOnce` now only gates duplicate dumps, resolves existing owned telemetry buffers, stages 300 unmanaged telemetry entries into the fixed snapshot, and queues the cached worker. `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` is the only filesystem method. Removed `KineticCharacterBlackBox` and the now-dead `System.IO` import from `KineticCharacterAnimatorTypes.cs`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the helper in the types file was rejected because it preserves a synchronous public disk IO route. Writing from `LateFrameTick` was rejected because that still stalls a frame phase on fault. Creating a dedicated `Thread` or process was rejected because worker lifetime must stay bounded; cached ThreadPool work plus `_blackBoxDumpInFlight` is enough. Adding a header to the dump was rejected because the old `Dump_KINETIC_ANIMATOR.bin` payload shape was raw telemetry records; this patch preserves record order and fields.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid one fault-time directory/file open and 300-entry binary write on the main runtime path. Middle devices preserve the telemetry payload and current kinetic animation behavior. High and Ultra devices retain continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling already present in tuning and job inputs for IK cadence/iteration quality, tool alignment, swim bob, wall brace, and GPU skinning constants. No binary `isLowEnd` switch and no physical body simulation was added; the animation remains deterministic procedural cheats over authored rig data.

The hot `Tick` path still owns at most one solver mutation guard mask and releases it in `finally` or completion cleanup; no DataVault write lock is acquired by the dump route. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because no Unity profiler/device run was performed.

## R95 - Procedural Ladder Climb Fault Dump Phase Split

Problem: `ProceduralLadderClimbRuntime.LateFrameTick` calls `DumpBlackBox` when the completed IK output contains non-finite values. The old `DumpBlackBox` route resolved telemetry views, created the dump directory if needed, opened `FileStream`, wrote an 8-byte header, and serialized up to 300 `LadderClimbTelemetryEntry` records directly on that late-frame fault path. The route did not hold a DataVault write lock, but it could still stall presentation after simulation completion.

Solution: Added a fixed `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, `_blackBoxDumpSnapshotCount`, `_blackBoxDumpInFlight`, and cached `WaitCallback`. `DumpBlackBox` now gates duplicate dumps, stages telemetry from existing `NativeArray` views into the fixed owner-local snapshot, and queues a cached ThreadPool worker. `WriteBlackBoxDumpWorker` clears `_blackBoxDumpInFlight` in `finally`. `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` is the only file persistence method and consumes only the staged snapshot plus scalar count. `PrepareBlackBoxDumpDirectoryCold` now catches specific IO/path exceptions instead of broad `Exception`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `FileStream` writes in `LateFrameTick` was rejected because fault persistence is not visual-sync work and can stall the frame when the system is already degraded. Creating a dedicated `Thread` or process was rejected because the project explicitly forbids orphan process risk; cached ThreadPool work with an in-flight gate is bounded. Allocating a fresh dump array per fault was rejected because the fault route must remain fixed-capacity. Acquiring a DataVault write lock for the dump was rejected because persistence is diagnostic readout, not truth mutation.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a fault-time file open and 300-entry write in the late-frame phase. Middle devices preserve the exact dump header and telemetry record order. High and Ultra devices keep the existing continuous quality-scaled ladder presentation: camera slide fake, grip pull, IK target smoothing, and head stabilization remain procedural cheats over cached transforms, not a physical climbing simulation and not a binary low-end/high-end switch.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault handling changed from directory check plus file open plus up to 300 serialized records to fixed unmanaged entry copies and one cached ThreadPool enqueue. Exact runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because no profiler/device run was performed.

## R96 - Biome Boundary Sample Lock Split And Fault Dump Worker

Problem: `BiomeBoundarySdfRuntime.SlowTick` used `TryAcquireSampleBuffers` to hold `SampleMutationGuardMask` across `BiomeBoundarySdfSampleJob.Execute()`. The sample result buffer was only a one-element local output slot, but it lived in `GlobalDataVault` and forced a broader mutation guard around read-only heatmap reads plus sample math. The same runtime also wrote the biome boundary blackbox dump synchronously from invalid-result fault handling.

Solution: Removed the runtime `BiomeBoundarySampleResult` DataVault route and replaced it with owner-local `_sampleResultScratch`, allocated once in cold native-storage setup and disposed with the runtime. `TryReadSampleInputs` now opens only two read-only DataVault views for the biome map/hash map; the sample job writes to local scratch. `BiomeBoundarySdfSampleJob` now consumes `NativeArray<byte>.ReadOnly` and `NativeArray<uint>.ReadOnly` inputs. Fault dump persistence now stages into a fixed `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, gates with `_blackBoxDumpInFlight`, and queues cached `s_blackBoxDumpWorker`; only `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` touches filesystem APIs.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the one-element sample result in DataVault was rejected because it is local scratch, not cross-domain truth. Holding `SampleMutationGuardMask` during scalar SDF sampling was rejected because the map is read-only during sample and the output does not require Vault authority. Allocating a managed array per fault was rejected because fault handling must stay fixed-capacity. Keeping synchronous disk write was rejected because fault persistence is diagnostic, not slow-tick simulation work.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a broad sample mutation guard and synchronous fault disk IO on the slow-tick path. Middle devices keep the same 256x256 heatmap semantics and one-sample biome gradient output. High and Ultra devices keep the existing cinematic cheat: a cheap local SDF/weight blend over a small map neighborhood, not a physical biome simulation and not a binary device switch. No gameplay truth ownership or DTO layout was changed.

Hardware Impact: The sample path changed from one DataVault mutation guard spanning job execution to two read-only handle checks and a local persistent `NativeArray<BiomeBoundarySdfResult>` write. Fault dump caller work changed from path/directory/file writes to fixed telemetry copy plus one ThreadPool enqueue. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because no profiler/device run was performed.

## R97 - Biome Transition Fault Dump Phase Split

Problem: `BiomeTransitionManagerRuntime.DumpBlackBox` was called from the invalid biome transition output path after `LateFrameTick` had settled pipeline publication. The old route resolved mutable telemetry/counter buffers and synchronously called `TryDumpTelemetry`, which performed project-root resolution, path construction, directory creation, file open, stack record serialization, and 300-entry binary writes in the runtime fault path. This was not a DataVault write-lock deadlock by itself, but it was a presentation-phase stall vector and kept disk IO adjacent to a fault path that should only snapshot state and return.

Solution: Added a fixed `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, `_blackBoxDumpSnapshotCount`, `_blackBoxDumpInFlight`, `_blackBoxDumpRootCold`, and cached `WaitCallback`. `DumpBlackBox` now uses read-only telemetry and counter views, stages a fixed snapshot, queues `WriteBlackBoxDumpWorker`, and never touches filesystem APIs. `WriteBlackBoxDumpWorker` clears the in-flight gate in `finally`; `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` is the only runtime file writer and consumes only staged scalar state. `TryEnsureBlackBoxDumpRootCold` caches project root during cold dependency resolution. Shader payload graphics buffers are also ensured from cold `Awake`/`OnEnable`; `PublishShaderPayload` now checks readiness instead of creating buffers in the publish path.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping synchronous `TryDumpTelemetry` in the runtime fault route was rejected because even guard-free disk IO can stall visual sync. Creating a dedicated `Thread` or external process was rejected because the task explicitly forbids orphan process risk; cached ThreadPool work plus an in-flight gate is bounded. Acquiring any DataVault write lock for dump persistence was rejected because a blackbox dump is diagnostic readout, not authority mutation. Removing the editor static dump helper was rejected because `TryDumpBlackBoxFromEditor` still intentionally provides a cold/editor diagnostic route.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a fault-time directory/file open and 300-entry write in the visual fault path. Middle devices preserve the exact binary telemetry record format and biome transition behavior. High and Ultra devices retain existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight`-driven biome shader payload scaling and fog/color blending; no binary low-end/high-end branch and no physical biome simulation was added. The cinematic cheat remains dithered mathematical blending over cached DTOs and a small shader payload, not a heavy environmental simulation.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault handling changed from path resolution plus binary serialization to bounded 300-entry struct copy and one cached ThreadPool queue attempt. The modified runtime dump path holds no DataVault write lock or mutation guard, and worker cleanup is `finally`-guarded. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because no Unity profiler/device run was performed and compile/parser gates were blocked by CPU policy.

## R98 - Sargassum Scavenger Matrix Write Lock Split

Problem: `SargassumGlobalDragManager.UpdateScavengerHosts` held `_scavengerMatricesHandle` write ownership across host iteration, chunk scavenger consumption, external-site aging, procedural orbit/bob math, bounds building, array clearing, debug state publication, and `GraphicsBufferUploadUtility.UploadNativeArray`. That made a visual matrix buffer behave like a broad simulation lock and kept GPU upload inside DataVault writer lifetime.

Solution: Added cold `_scavengerMatrixStaging` capacity beside the existing matrix Vault buffer. `UpdateScavengerHosts` now computes procedural scavenger matrices into the staging array without holding a Vault writer. `TryPublishScavengerMatrices` is the only matrix writer and copies staged matrices into `_scavengerMatricesHandle` under one bounded `TryAcquireWriteLock`, released in `finally`. `UploadScavengerInstances` reopens a read-only view after the writer is released and performs GPU upload outside write ownership. Active host/external counts are committed before fail-closed visual publish return so chunk/local procedural state stays consistent even when matrix presentation is skipped.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping GPU upload under the writer lock was rejected because GraphicsBuffer upload is presentation IO, not DataVault authority. Allocating a managed list or array per frame was rejected; `_scavengerMatrixStaging` is cold-capacity storage released with scavenger buffers. Creating a tiny job for matrix copy was rejected because same-frame schedule/readback for a simple contiguous copy has no profiler proof. Reverting chunk consumption on visual publish failure was rejected because matrix publication is visual output; the chunk owner state has already advanced and should not be tied to a presentation buffer.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a long writer lock around cheap procedural visual math and GPU upload. Middle devices keep the same cardinal scavenger orbit/bob fake and instance counts. High and Ultra devices keep visual overkill through existing continuous counts, scale ranges, bob amplitude, and orbit radius; no binary low-end/high-end branch and no physical scavenger simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: The critical `_scavengerMatricesHandle` writer span changed from a 134-line mixed simulation/presentation block to a 33-line copy-only method with one counted loop and guaranteed `finally` release. Exact runtime microseconds remain unmeasured because no profiler/device run was performed and compile/parser gates were blocked by CPU policy.

## R99 - Construction Socket Mock Generation Lock Flattening

Problem: `ShinobuSocketConstructionRuntime.GenerateMockBaseConstructionGrid` acquired six write lanes at once: modules, socket states, socket AUPs, counters, CSR ranges, and CSR target indices. It then built the whole mock construction grid while all six lanes remained locked. This violated the one-writer-lock-at-a-time rule even though the caller is an editor/cold tool route.

Solution: Added cold static scratch arrays for each mock lane. The mock grid is generated entirely into scratch first. Before publishing, `TryInvalidateCounterLane` clears `ConstructionSocketCounters` under a single write lock so readers fail closed during partial publication. `TryPublishWriteLane` then publishes one lane at a time, each with one lock and one `finally` release. Final counters, including `CounterMagic`, are published last.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping all six lanes locked was rejected because it couples independent DataVault ownership and creates a deadlock-shaped pattern. Publishing counters first was rejected because readers could accept valid magic over partially refreshed buffers. Allocating temporary arrays per call was rejected; the route now uses cold static scratch sized to constants. Introducing a job was rejected because this is editor/cold mock data generation and would add scheduling complexity without runtime frame proof.

Scalability potential: Low devices/editor sessions avoid a broad DataVault writer chain during mock construction setup. Middle devices keep the same 500-module / 3000-socket mock grid. High and Ultra devices retain the same deterministic socket CSR and compatibility layout; no binary tier switch and no physical construction simulation was introduced.

Hardware Impact: Max simultaneous DataVault write ownership in the modified mock-generation route changed from six lanes to one lane. Publish order now provides a fail-closed counter sentinel. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because no profiler/device run was performed and compile/parser gates were blocked by CPU policy.
