## R100 - VRSomatic Comfort And Horizon Lock Flattening

Problem: `VRSomaticProvider.Comfort` and `VRSomaticProvider.HorizonLock` held multiple `GlobalDataVault` write lanes while scheduling jobs that consumed unsafe pointers into those lanes. Release occurred immediately after scheduling, which meant writer ownership ended before scheduled work was guaranteed to finish. The same route also held telemetry write locks while invoking `DumpComfortTelemetryOnce`, which performs filesystem IO and read-only telemetry walks. A second defect existed in dump routing: comfort telemetry and blackbox dumps both targeted `Dump_1335_SomaticComfort.bin`, so two incompatible binary formats could overwrite the same artifact.

Solution: Replaced the scheduled comfort/horizon route with immediate scalar execution of the existing Burst job structs against stack-local DTOs. Final DTOs are published through `TryPublishFirst`, which owns exactly one write lane and releases in `finally`. Buffer seeding now writes each lane sequentially through one-lane helpers; profile/lookup/mock sample population each has a single writer. Horizon lock evaluation now reads prior state through read-only views, computes locally, and publishes KCC mirror, raw rotation, and horizon state one lane at a time. Telemetry recorders now only set a `shouldDump` scalar while locked; dump calls occur after the writer is released. Comfort telemetry dump filename is split to `Dump_1335_SomaticComfortTelemetry.bin`; blackbox keeps `Dump_1335_SomaticComfort.bin`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping scheduled jobs with Vault pointers was rejected because a `JobHandle` dependency is not ownership proof after the write lock is released. Holding all comfort/horizon lanes until completion was rejected because it serializes unrelated buffers and blocks defrag/sibling systems. Adding new tiny jobs for copy/seed/clear was rejected because the affected lanes are single-row or fixed small telemetry buffers and scheduling cost is not justified. Keeping the shared dump filename was rejected because one route writes comfort+horizon telemetry rows while the blackbox route writes `VRSomaticBlackBoxEntry` rows.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid writer starvation and same-frame job/readback churn in the VR comfort path. Middle devices retain the same comfort state, foveated multiplier, horizon stabilization, and mock sickness semantics. High and Ultra devices still use continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` in derivative stride, profile smoothing, FOV tunnel intensity, horizon spring precision, mock motion amplitude, and exact-vs-cheap critical damping blend. No binary `isLowEnd` switch and no physical vestibular simulation was introduced; the system remains a controllable cinematic cheat using scalar approximations.

Hardware Impact: Worst writer topology in the edited VRSomatic comfort route changed from thirteen simultaneous write lanes during seed and four-to-six write lanes during comfort/horizon scheduling to one write lane per helper call. The fault dump path no longer runs under telemetry writer ownership. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because the final resource gate had `CPU_SAMPLE=100.000000`, `dotnet=1`, and `csc=1`.

## R101 - VRSomatic BlackBox Fault Dump Phase Split

Problem: `VRSomaticProvider.RecordBlackBoxFrame` is called from `Tick` and wrote the blackbox entry under a DataVault writer before immediately entering `DumpBlackBoxOnce` on non-finite state. The old dump route resolved `Application.dataPath`, built paths, created directories, opened a `FileStream`, and repeatedly reopened read-only blackbox views through `TryReadBlackBoxEntry`. A second hot-path violation existed in the same recorder: when the blackbox buffer was missing, it called `DispatcherJobSwap.TryFinalizeCompleted` from the Tick-reachable path, even though that helper is contractually a dispatcher swap or teardown window tool.

Solution: Added a fixed `_blackBoxDumpSnapshot`, cached cold path fields, `_blackBoxDumpInFlight`, pending fault fields, and a cached `WaitCallback`. `RecordBlackBoxFrame` now releases the blackbox writer in `finally` before calling `DumpBlackBoxOnce` and no longer finalizes job handles. `DumpBlackBoxOnce` only gates duplicate dumps, stages the fixed snapshot through one read-only blackbox view, and queues the cached worker. `TryWriteBlackBoxSnapshotCold` is the only file writer and consumes the fixed snapshot; worker failure is staged with interlocked fields and flushed from owner Tick through a cheap `Volatile.Read` guard. Snapshot count publication uses `Volatile.Write` and worker consumption uses `Volatile.Read`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping direct filesystem writes in the fault route was rejected because fault persistence must not stall simulation/presentation after non-finite detection. Allocating a fresh managed array per dump was rejected because blackbox evidence is fixed-capacity. Reopening the DataVault read-only view for every dumped row was rejected because the snapshot can be copied once before worker handoff. Keeping `TryFinalizeCompleted` in `RecordBlackBoxFrame` was rejected because a blackbox record call must not touch job fences from Tick.

Scalability potential: Low devices reduce the non-finite fault path to one writer release, bounded struct copy, and one queued worker attempt; no directory or file open occurs on the main fault path. Middle devices preserve the same 300-frame blackbox binary format. High and Ultra devices preserve the existing continuous VRSomatic scaling: comfort vignette, dynamic horizon lock, condensation, near-field collision, and hand ghost tolerance continue to consume `GlobalQualityWeight` as scalar fidelity, not a binary low-end/high-end switch. The blackbox change buys stability without changing gameplay truth, DTO layout, save identity, or authority route.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault persistence changed from path resolution plus directory/file creation plus per-row read-only Vault reopening to a fixed 300-entry owner-local copy and one cached ThreadPool enqueue. Estimated low-end gain is avoiding an unbounded filesystem stall in the already-degraded non-finite path; exact microseconds remain unmeasured because no Unity profiler/device run was performed. Compilation proof was a single throttled build after `CPU_SAMPLE=8`, `dotnet=0`, `csc=0`, `vbc=0`.

## R102 - Bootstrap Shader Warmup Dump Cache And Exosuit Telemetry Publish Split

Problem: `GameBootstrapper.QueueBootstrapShaderWarmupTelemetryDump` still resolved dump paths, ensured the parent directory, and could allocate its dump scratch from the failure path before staging telemetry. After my first pass, a cold path-resolution failure could still be retried by later fault dump requests. `ExosuitKinematicsRuntime.CompletePendingJob` also published `GlobalTelemetryBus` dump events while the job mutation guard was still held, because `EmitReadbackSignals` called the dump route before `UnlockJobBuffers`.

Solution: `GameBootstrapper` now owns a fixed cold `byte[19200]` shader warmup dump scratch and a one-shot `_shaderWarmupDumpPathCacheAttempted` guard. The failure queue path only reads cached strings, copies telemetry under one writer, releases in `finally`, then queues the worker. Parent directory creation moved to the worker through `TryEnsureBootstrapShaderWarmupDumpDirectoryCold`. `ExosuitKinematicsRuntime` now stages the latest telemetry scalar/hash under the job guard, releases `UnlockJobBuffers()` in `finally`, and only then calls `PublishTelemetryDump`.

Rejected Alternatives: Retrying path resolution from the fault queue was rejected because repeated failures would reintroduce managed path work into the diagnostic route. Holding Exosuit job ownership while calling `GlobalTelemetryBus.TryDumpBlackboxNow` was rejected because blackbox publication is diagnostic output, not simulation authority. Copying the full Exosuit telemetry ring was rejected because the global blackbox API only needs the event scalar and state hash.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid path work and directory IO on a shader-warmup failure path and avoid telemetry-bus publication under Exosuit job ownership. Middle devices preserve identical shader warmup telemetry and Exosuit blackbox event payloads. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` driven shader warmup budgeting and Exosuit solver quality paths; no binary device switch and no physical over-simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Bootstrap failure handling changed from path resolution plus possible directory IO plus conditional scratch allocation to one cached-path read, fixed byte copy, and queued worker. Exosuit fault/budget dump publication no longer executes while the mutation guard is held. Exact microseconds remain unmeasured because the compile/profiler gate stayed above 50% CPU.

## R103 - Ecosystem Biomass Overflow Telemetry Publish Split

Problem: `EcosystemDirector.TryQueueBiomassImpact` published `GlobalTelemetryBus.PublishPerformanceWarning` while `_pendingBiomassImpacts` write ownership was still held. The warning is diagnostic/presentation telemetry, not biomass queue authority, so it extended the DataVault writer with a global bus call.

Solution: Kept the bounded queue mutation under one write lock and one `finally` release. Overflow now records `publishOverflowWarning` plus `overflowCount` while locked, releases `_pendingBiomassImpacts`, then publishes the warning after the lock is gone. Return semantics remain fail-closed: overflow still returns `false`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping telemetry inside the lock was rejected because the bus path is not the queue owner. Dropping the warning was rejected because saturation evidence is useful. Allocating a separate event object was rejected; scalar staging is enough.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a global telemetry call while an ecosystem write lane is owned. Middle devices preserve biomass overflow visibility. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous biomass and macro-swarm scaling; this patch changes no gameplay truth, DTO layout, or quality-tier behavior.

## R104 - Seaglide Runtime Vault Mutation Guard Flattening

Problem: `SeaglideHydrodynamicsRuntime` had several DataVault sovereignty violations in one cluster. Editor CSV import used a `GlobalDataVault` byte lane as transient scratch and wrote tuning through raw mutable array resolution. `FixedTick` resolved mutable Vault arrays, then mutated requests, states, tuning, telemetry, counters, and force packets before acquiring the job mutation guard. `PostFixedTick` and presentation paths passed mutable arrays into routines that only read. Completion diagnostics updated counter and telemetry lanes after releasing the solver guard, then could continue into blackbox/signal publication.

Solution: Removed the Seaglide CSV scratch Vault handle and moved CSV bytes to bounded stack scratch. CSV parse now happens before the only tuning write lock; `TryLoadVehicleProfileCsvCold` commits one DTO through `TryAcquireWriteLock` and releases in `finally`. `FixedTick` now acquires `TryAcquireJobBufferGuard` before any mutable runtime buffer write and releases in `finally` if no job is scheduled; scheduled jobs keep the same guard until `FinishPendingSolverCompletion` releases it. `PostFixedTick`, presentation publishing, fault check, and blackbox event routing now use `TryReadOnlyVaultBuffer`. `PhysicsApplySystem.DrainSeaglideForcePackets` and its body resolver helper accept read-only packet/counter/binding lanes. Completion telemetry writers reacquire the existing mutation guard for the bounded counter/telemetry mutation and release before diagnostics. Cold boot, body binding, editor mock generation, and fallback heartbeat writes now have explicit guarded spans; cold tuning seed uses one write lock released in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `CsvScratch` in GlobalDataVault was rejected because transient import bytes are not cross-domain truth. Replacing heartbeat/counter/cursor writes with multiple independent write locks was rejected because those lanes form one owner-local simulation packet and the existing mutation guard already covers the complete lane set without nested write-lock topology. Keeping raw mutable arrays in read-only drains was rejected because it makes future write creep invisible. Starting another build while external PID `21592` was compiling was rejected by the CPU/compiler throttle rule.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid unnecessary Vault scratch ownership and avoid unguarded writer races that can become stalls under compaction or contention. Middle devices keep the same seaglide cadence, propulsion, audio, cavitation, and blackbox behavior. High and Ultra devices keep continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling through cadence, presentation signal budget, cavitation intensity, and solver fidelity; no binary low-end/high-end branch and no physical over-simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Runtime mutation ownership in `FixedTick` is now one explicit guard covering all mutable seaglide simulation lanes instead of an unowned pre-guard write window. Post-fixed force drain and visual/audio publication now read immutable snapshots and hold no writer. Exact microseconds are unmeasured; expected low-end gain is reduced contention and no transient CSV Vault lane. Final compile after the guarded patch was blocked by existing external `dotnet build Hecton8.slnx` PID `21592`.

## R105 - Parasite Swarm Telemetry Dump Guard Split

Problem: `ParasiteSwarmGpuRuntime.LateFrameTick` held `TelemetryMutationGuardMask` while it copied the full 300-entry telemetry ring into the managed blackbox snapshot. The copy is diagnostic state transfer, not the authoritative telemetry mutation itself. Holding the mutation guard across the ring copy inflated the owner-lane span and made future dump work easier to accidentally move under the guard.

Solution: Kept `RecordTelemetry` as the only writer under `TryAcquireTelemetryOwnerViews`. The dump decision is now staged as a bool while locked; `ReleaseTelemetryOwnerViews` runs in `finally`; `TryStageTelemetryDump` reopens telemetry ring/cursor through `TryReadHandle` and copies the snapshot in `TryStageTelemetryDumpFromReadOnly` without any DataVault writer or mutation guard. Worker handoff now publishes snapshot count with `Volatile.Write` after cursor/root assignment, and the worker consumes count/cursor with `Volatile.Read`.

Rejected Alternatives: Copying the ring under the mutation guard was rejected because it expands diagnostic work inside owner mutation. Adding another write lock for the snapshot was rejected because the snapshot is local blackbox evidence, not shared Vault truth. Allocating a fresh dump array was rejected because the class already owns a fixed `SwarmTelemetryEntry[TelemetryCapacity]` snapshot. Moving file IO into `LateFrameTick` was rejected; the worker path remains the persistence boundary.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid a 300-entry snapshot copy while telemetry owner lanes are held. Middle devices preserve identical parasite telemetry dump payloads. High and Ultra devices keep the existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling of particle budget, mock target intensity, curl/noise, and GPU workload; no binary tier branch and no physical parasite simulation was introduced.

Hardware Impact: Mutation ownership in the parasite telemetry dump path now covers only ring/cursor mutation. Snapshot copy and worker enqueue happen after release. Exact microseconds are unmeasured; targeted compile passed.

## R106 - Fabrication Assembler Telemetry Writer And Dump Split

Problem: `FabricationAssemblerRuntime.PostSimulationTick` wrote `ShinobuFabricationTelemetryRing` through a mutable Vault view and, on fault flags, immediately copied the same ring into `_telemetryDumpSnapshot` through `QueueTelemetryDump(telemetry, faultFlags)`. The dump copy is diagnostic state transfer, not telemetry authority. The route also lacked an explicit write-lock boundary around the telemetry ring write, making ownership proof weak.

Solution: `PostSimulationTick` now computes telemetry from read-only fabrication job/runtime lanes first. `TryWriteTelemetryEntry` then acquires exactly one write lock on `BufferID.ShinobuFabricationTelemetryRing`, writes one entry and the cursor, releases in `finally`, and returns a scalar dump decision. `QueueTelemetryDump` is called after release, reopens telemetry through read-only `TryReadHandle`, copies into the fixed snapshot, writes frame/reason, then publishes count with `Volatile.Write` before queueing the cached worker.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping snapshot copy on the mutable telemetry view was rejected because it expands diagnostic work inside the writer span. Reusing `TryOpenWriteArray` inside the new writer was rejected after review because that helper resolves `_vault` internally; acquire and release for this path now use one local `IDataVault` instance. Allocating a fresh dump array was rejected because `_telemetryDumpSnapshot` is already fixed at `TelemetryFrameCount`.

Scalability potential: Low devices now hold the telemetry writer only for a single entry/cursor write. Middle devices preserve the same 300-frame telemetry dump payload. High and Ultra devices retain existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling of shader upload quality, edge glow, and simulation budgets; no binary device switch and no physical fabrication simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread fault telemetry path changed from mutable-ring write plus 300-entry snapshot copy on the same mutable view to one short write lock followed by read-only snapshot copy. Exact microseconds are unmeasured because compile/profiler gate was blocked by active external build and CPU load.

## R107 - Ground Penetrating Radar Fault Telemetry Single Write

That doubled `BufferID.GroundRadarTelemetryRing` write-lock acquisition on the fault success path.

Solution: Folded fault detection into a scalar `hasTelemetryFault` before telemetry write. The successful publish path now writes exactly one telemetry row with either `0u` or `TelemetryFaultFlag`, then calls `DumpBlackBox` after `WriteTelemetry` has returned and released its write lock.

Holding the telemetry writer while dumping was rejected; existing dump staging stays read-only and worker queued. Creating a separate fault event lane was rejected because this domain already has a blackbox dump and telemetry flag bit.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid one extra DataVault writer acquisition on GPR non-finite fault frames. Middle devices preserve the same radar scan and blackbox payload. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous scan quality and visual upload behavior; no binary low-end switch and no physical subsurface simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Fault success path writer acquisitions changed from two `WriteTelemetry` calls to one. Exact microseconds are unmeasured because compile/profiler gate was blocked by active external build and high CPU.

## R108 - Cartography BlackBox VisualSync Dump Split

Problem: `PlayerExplorationTracker.DumpCartographyBlackBox` acquired cartography telemetry pins and called `CartographyVault.TryDumpBlackBox` while those pins were held. That call built dump paths, created the `Docs/AgentLogs` directory, copied the telemetry ring, and queued a `ThreadPool` worker inside the same mutation-guard span. The ring copy is bounded evidence staging; path and worker scheduling are diagnostic presentation/persistence and must not extend DataVault ownership.

Solution: Split cartography dump into three stages. `DumpCartographyBlackBox` now only stages the fixed telemetry snapshot while `CartographyPinTelemetryRing | CartographyPinTelemetryCursor` is held, releases pins in `finally`, then marks `_cartographyBlackBoxDumpStaged`. `CartographyVisualSyncTick` flushes that staged dump after post-simulation through `FlushStagedCartographyBlackBoxDump`. `CartographyVault.TryStageBlackBoxSnapshot` copies the fixed 300-entry ring with no file IO and no worker queue; `TryQueueStagedBlackBoxDump` sets the path and queues the cached callback without any Vault lock; `WriteTelemetryDump` performs directory/file IO on the worker and clears `telemetryDumpPending` in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `TryDumpBlackBox` monolithic was rejected because diagnostic file prep and worker enqueue were inside the telemetry mutation guard. Copying the telemetry ring after releasing pins from raw Vault arrays was rejected because the buffer view is not safe after mutation ownership ends. Retrying queue failures every frame was rejected; `_cartographyDumpedThisSession` still flips once after a resolved dump attempt. Adding a new DataVault lane for dump snapshots was rejected because blackbox evidence is owner-local fixed memory, not cross-domain truth.

Scalability potential: Low devices now hold cartography telemetry pins only for a bounded 300-entry struct copy and not for path resolution, directory creation, or worker scheduling. Middle devices preserve the same `Dump_1321_Cartography.bin` binary payload. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling of cartography sample cadence, POI reveal cadence, map upload cadence, scanner radius, resolution, and glow; no binary low-end switch and no physical scan simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Main-thread mutation ownership changed from snapshot copy plus path/Directory/ThreadPool work under pins to only fixed snapshot copy under pins, then VisualSync queue outside ownership. Exact microseconds are unmeasured because CPU stayed above the compile/profiler gate.

## R109 - Ecosystem Flora Predator AUP Publish Lock Split

Problem: `EcosystemDirector.PublishFloraPredatorAupBufferImmediate` acquired the `EcosystemFloraPredatorAupUpload` write lane and, when the acquired upload buffer was not created, called `PublishFloraPredatorAupGlobalsImmediate(0)` and `PublishApexPresenceFakeImmediate(IsApexInSector(queryOrigin))` before the `finally` released the writer. Those helper calls drive shader globals and ocean panic presentation state; they are not the owner write to the Vault upload lane and must not run under a DataVault writer.

Solution: Added a scalar `publishEmptyFallback` flag. The invalid-buffer branch now only sets that flag while the writer is owned. The `finally` releases `_floraPredatorAupUpload`, then the fallback shader/apex publish runs outside the writer span and returns. The valid path still fills the Vault upload lane and fixed `float4[]` snapshot under one write lock; graphics upload, telemetry saturation warning, and shader global publish remain after the release in `LateFrameTick` visual flush.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping immediate publish in the invalid-buffer branch was rejected because it extends a DataVault writer across shader/global side effects. Skipping the fallback publish was rejected because predators must still clear the shader count and apex panic flag when the upload lane is unavailable. Adding a new event or queue allocation was rejected because one scalar flag is sufficient and zero-GC.

Scalability potential: Low devices avoid shader/global presentation work while a Vault writer is owned and keep a bounded 32-entry upload lane. Middle devices preserve the existing visual fake: predator stealth remains a cheap AUP StructuredBuffer and global shader parameters, not a physical predator-field simulation. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` ecosystem scaling; this patch does not introduce binary device branches or alter gameplay truth.

Hardware Impact: The invalid-upload fallback writer span now contains only `TryAcquireWriteLock`, an `IsCreated` check, scalar flag write, and `ReleaseWriteLock`. Exact microseconds are unmeasured; the expected low-end gain is reduced worst-case writer contention during VisualSync.

Middle devices keep the same nutrient and carrion CSV authoring behavior. High and Ultra devices keep the existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` scaling for nutrient active axis, interpolation, falloff, upload cadence, carrion decay, attraction, and nutrient injection; no binary quality branch and no physical chemistry simulation was introduced.

## R111 - Physiology CSV Scratch DataVault Removal

Problem: `ShinobuSensoryImpairmentRuntime` and `ShinobuRadiationMutationRuntime` used DataVault CSV scratch buffers for transient editor import bytes, then kept `CsvImportMutationGuardMask` around file read/parsing work before committing profile and tuning DTOs. That made cold authoring I/O part of a GlobalDataVault mutation span and treated temporary CSV bytes as shared truth.

Solution: Removed the CSV scratch handles and scratch BufferID participation from both runtimes. Each import route now uses fixed static cold scratch arrays for CSV bytes and parsed DTOs, protected by a scalar `Interlocked.CompareExchange` busy flag. `ReadCsvBytesCold` and `ParseProfilesCsv` run before the mutation guard. The guard now covers only resolving profile/tuning buffers, copying parsed DTOs into Vault-owned arrays, updating tuning, and releasing in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping transient CSV bytes in `GlobalDataVault` was rejected because the bytes are editor/cold import staging, not cross-domain runtime ownership. Holding the mutation guard across `FileStream` was rejected because disk I/O and CSV parse latency do not belong inside a Vault mutation lane. Allocating per-import managed scratch was rejected because the existing pattern can be fixed with bounded static arrays and one busy flag.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor-on-laptop workflows avoid disk I/O and parse work while a Vault mutation guard is owned. Middle devices preserve identical CSV authoring behavior. High and Ultra devices keep existing profile-driven visual/gameplay response; no binary quality switch and no physical physiology simulation was added. `GlobalQualityWeight` behavior was not changed because this is cold data import ownership, not scalable runtime fidelity.

Hardware Impact: Mutation ownership now covers only DTO commit into profile/tuning lanes. Exact microseconds are unmeasured; structural gain is removal of `FileStream` and parse loops from `CsvImportMutationGuardMask` spans in two physiology runtimes. Compile proof remains blocked by CPU policy.

## R112 - Battery Charger Profile CSV DataVault Scratch Removal

Problem: `BatteryChargerLogisticsRuntime.MonitorProfileCsv` acquired `CsvImportMutationGuardMask`, resolved a DataVault byte scratch buffer, then performed `FileStream` read and CSV parse before writing charger profiles. The public editor `TryLoadProfilesFromCsvBytes` also parsed directly into the Vault profile buffer while the profile import guard was held. The contracts allocated `BatteryChargerLogisticsBufferIds.CsvScratch` as if transient CSV import bytes were shared runtime truth.

Solution: Removed `CsvScratch` from `BatteryChargerLogisticsHandles`, `EnsureBuffers`, and readiness checks. Added fixed editor-only static byte/profile scratch arrays in the runtime with one `Interlocked.CompareExchange` busy flag. Both editor import routes parse into static profile scratch before acquiring `ProfileImportMutationGuardMask`. The guard now covers only resolving `Profiles`, copying DTOs through `CommitProfilesCsv`, clearing stale profile slots, and releasing in `finally`. Added a `Span<ChargerProfileDTO>` parser overload so parsing can happen outside the Vault-owned `NativeArray`.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the byte scratch in DataVault was rejected because CSV import bytes are not cross-domain authority state. Holding the guard during `FileStream` read was rejected because disk latency is unrelated to profile ownership. Parsing directly into the Vault profile array was rejected because malformed CSV should fail before a profile lane is owned. Allocating a per-call profile array was rejected; static editor scratch is bounded and guarded.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor-on-laptop workflows avoid file IO and parse loops while profile mutation ownership is held. Middle devices preserve the same CSV profile authoring route. High and Ultra devices retain existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` use in battery charger cadence and telemetry; no binary device branch, no extra simulation, and no gameplay truth route change was introduced.

Hardware Impact: Removes one obsolete Vault byte lane and shrinks two editor profile import guard spans to DTO commit only. Exact runtime microseconds are unmeasured; compile proof is clean.

## R113 - Suit Integrity Cold Initialization And Dump Scratch Split

Problem: `ShinobuSuitIntegrityRuntime` still carried Vault CSV/dump scratch handles for transient editor bytes and diagnostic output staging. It also initialized default tuning/profile/state lanes and generated mock hydrostatic AUP data through direct Vault array opens without an explicit owner mutation guard, which made cold setup code an unsafe pattern for later runtime systems to copy.

Solution: Removed CSV and dump scratch handles from the runtime. CSV import now uses fixed static cold byte/profile scratch with a scalar busy flag; parsing completes before `ProfileCsvMutationGuardMask`, and the guard covers only profile commit plus last-write tick update. Default profile/state setup now builds default DTOs into static scratch before acquiring `DefaultsMutationGuardMask`, commits tuning/profiles/states inside one guarded span, releases in `finally`, then calls the separate CSV and mock AUP routes after release. Mock hydrostatic data now builds fixed scratch before `MockAupMutationGuardMask` and commits only the mock AUP lane under that guard. Autopsy dump staging reads telemetry through read-only Vault arrays and writes the worker payload outside any Vault mutation/write ownership.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping CSV or dump bytes in `GlobalDataVault` was rejected because they are transient editor/diagnostic staging, not cross-domain truth. Nesting profile CSV or mock AUP mutation under default initialization was rejected because it would hold multiple mutation lanes in one call stack. Directly mutating default and mock Vault arrays was rejected even though the path is cold; cold code defines the contract other systems copy.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor-on-laptop workflows avoid file/dump scratch lanes and keep cold setup mutation spans bounded to DTO copies. Middle devices preserve the same suit pressure profile authoring and mock AUP defaults. High and Ultra devices keep existing profile-driven suit integrity behavior; no binary low-end switch and no physical suit pressure simulation were added. `GlobalQualityWeight` is not changed because this pass fixes ownership and lock duration, not visual fidelity scaling.

Hardware Impact: Removes two obsolete Vault scratch lanes from suit integrity and changes default/mock cold setup from unguarded direct writes to bounded guarded commits. Exact runtime microseconds are unmeasured; compile proof is clean.

## R122 - Ecosystem Balancer CSV And Legacy Scratch Vault Lane Removal

Problem: `ShinobuEcosystemBalancer` still claimed three transient byte lanes in GlobalDataVault: `ShinobuSpatialGridCsvScratch`, `ShinobuEcosystemCsvScratch`, and `ShinobuEcosystemLegacyScratch`. Current editor CSV and legacy routes already stage bytes in managed cold scratch and parse via `ReadOnlySpan<byte>` before committing DTOs, so the Vault byte handles were dead ownership routes. `TryResolveSpatialGridBuffers` was also private and uncalled, and existed only to expose the dead spatial CSV scratch view.

Solution: Removed `_spatialGridCsvScratchHandle`, `_csvScratchHandle`, `_legacyScratchHandle`, their `ClaimVaultHandle<byte>` calls, readiness checks, reset paths, release calls, and the unused `TryResolveSpatialGridBuffers` method. Kept dump snapshot byte lanes because those are forensic payloads, not CSV scratch. Kept global `H8Memory.BufferID` enum values because enum cleanup is a coordinated API change and this patch only proves no runtime script route consumes these three IDs.

Rejected Alternatives: Removing the public enum values was rejected because global BufferID values can be referenced by external route cards or serialized data outside this runtime file. Moving CSV bytes back into NativeArray scratch was rejected because transient authoring bytes are not runtime truth. Broad parser deletion was rejected because some NativeArray parser overloads are public/static compatibility surface; the confirmed defect was stale Vault ownership, not the parser API.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor laptops avoid three obsolete Vault byte lanes, three readiness probes, and three release probes. Middle devices preserve identical ecosystem/spatial/swarm CSV authoring behavior. High and Ultra devices keep existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` flocking, spatial grid, and visual upload cadence; no binary quality switch and no physical ecosystem simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Removes one spatial CSV byte lane, one ecosystem CSV byte lane, and one legacy profile byte lane from Vault ownership. Structural savings are cold readiness/release work and lower alias table pressure; exact runtime microseconds are unmeasured. Targeted compile is clean.

## R123 - Voxel AStar Profile CSV Scratch And Guard Split

Problem: `PathFunnelNavmeshRuntime_VoxelAStar` still allocated `BufferID.ShinobuVoxelPathCsvScratch`, exposed `_voxelPathCsvScratchHandle`, and serialized a CSV scratch byte size even though `TryLoadVoxelPathingProfiles` already receives CSV bytes as `ReadOnlySpan<byte>`. The same method parsed directly into the Vault `VoxelPathingProfileDTO` array while holding the profile/count mutation guard.

Solution: Removed the unused CSV scratch byte lane from fields, capacity resolver, Vault ensure, release, and reset paths. Added a `Span<VoxelPathingProfileDTO>` parser overload in `VoxelAStarJobs.cs`. `TryLoadVoxelPathingProfiles` now parses into stack scratch before acquiring `VoxelPathProfileMutationGuardMask`, then the guarded span copies staged DTOs into the Vault profile lane, clears stale rows, writes the count, and releases in `finally`.

Rejected Alternatives: Removing `BufferID.ShinobuVoxelPathCsvScratch` from `H8Memory` was rejected because global enum cleanup is a coordinated API pass. Keeping direct parse into the Vault profile array was rejected because CSV parsing is cold authoring work and does not belong inside the profile/count ownership span. Changing the existing NativeArray parser signature was rejected because it is public editor compatibility surface; a new overload keeps compatibility.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor laptops avoid one obsolete Vault byte lane and parse no CSV while holding the mutation guard. Middle devices keep the same fauna pathing profile authoring route. High and Ultra retain existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` pathing cadence and A* time-slice behavior; no binary tier branch and no heavier physical path simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Removes one up-to-64 KiB editor CSV scratch lane from Vault ownership and reduces profile guard work to DTO copy/clear/count only. Exact runtime microseconds are unmeasured. Targeted compile is clean.

## R124 - Base Atmosphere Profile CSV Guard Split

Problem: `BaseAtmosphereLogisticsRuntime` kept an editor-only `AtmosphereLogisticsCsvScratch` byte buffer in GlobalDataVault and used it as CSV import staging. `MonitorGasProfileCsv` then parsed directly into the Vault `Profiles` array and wrote `Tuning` without a profile/tuning mutation guard. This made transient authoring bytes look like cross-domain authority and left profile/tuning commit ownership implicit.

Solution: Removed `_csvScratch` from runtime fields, ensure, release, clear, and owned-buffer checks; removed `AtmosphereLogisticsBufferIds.CsvScratch` from the local buffer contract. `MonitorGasProfileCsv` now stages bytes in `stackalloc byte[CsvScratchBytes]`, parses into `Span<AtmosphereGasProfileDTO>`, then acquires `ProfileCsvMutationGuardMask` for the final `Profiles`/`Tuning` copy only. Added a span parser overload while keeping the existing NativeArray overload for editor compatibility.

Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the byte lane in GlobalDataVault was rejected because editor CSV bytes are not runtime truth. Holding the guard across `File.Exists`, `GetLastWriteTimeUtc`, file read, and parser work was rejected because disk and parser latency do not belong in a Vault ownership span. Removing `BufferID.AtmosphereLogisticsCsvScratch` from the global enum was rejected because global enum cleanup is a coordinated API pass.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor laptops avoid one obsolete 16 KiB Vault lane and no longer mutate profile/tuning state without guard proof. Middle devices preserve the same gas profile authoring behavior. High and Ultra retain existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` diffusion iteration scaling and shader payload cadence; no binary tier branch and no heavier physical gas simulation was added.

Hardware Impact: Removes one editor CSV byte lane from Vault readiness/release work and bounds CSV commit ownership to 64 DTO copies plus one tuning write. Exact runtime microseconds are unmeasured. Targeted compile is clean.

## R125 - Kinetic Character Editor Profile Guard Split

Problem: `KineticCharacterAnimatorRuntime` editor routes wrote `Tuning` and `Rigs` directly after CSV parse. The Vault scratch byte compatibility API was public, but the actual profile/tuning commit had no dedicated mutation guard. `TryApplyEditorTuning` also wrote tuning through a private mutable accessor without a guard.

Solution: Added `EditorTuningMutationGuardMask` for direct editor tuning writes and `EditorProfileMutationGuardMask` for CSV profile commits. `TryApplyEditorTuning` now acquires/releases the tuning guard in `finally`. `TryApplyCsvProfile` and `TryApplyCsvProfileBytes` read current DTOs and parse into local structs first, then call `TryCommitEditorProfile`, which resolves `Tuning` and `Rigs` under one bounded guard. `TryApplyCsvProfileFromVaultScratch` remains as a compatibility bridge but its final write now routes through the guarded bytes path.

Rejected Alternatives: Removing `KineticCharacterAnimatorBufferIds.CsvScratch` was rejected because `TryApplyCsvProfileFromVaultScratch(int)` is public editor compatibility surface. Holding the guard during CSV parsing was rejected because parse latency is cold authoring work, not ownership. Rewriting `GenerateEmergencyMockRig` in this pass was rejected because it is broader lifecycle initialization and not required to guard the editor CSV/profile commit defect.

Scalability potential: Low devices and editor laptops avoid unguarded tuning/rig writes during authoring. Middle devices keep the same CSV profile bridge. High and Ultra keep the existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` IK iteration/tuning path; no binary tier branch and no physical body simulation expansion was added.

Hardware Impact: Guarded commit now covers two DTO writes instead of letting editor parse routes write shared Vault state directly. Exact runtime microseconds are unmeasured. Targeted compile is clean.
